The deal included a 76% stake in [Fox Kids Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Kids_Europe), Latin American channel [Fox Kids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Kids), more than 6,500 episodes from [Saban Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saban_Entertainment)'s programming library, and [Fox Family Channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Family_Channel).[^cite_note-292] It also bought the rights to the [Winnie-the-Pooh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winnie-the-Pooh).[^cite_note-293] In 2001, Disney's operations had a net loss of \$158 million after a decline in viewership of the ABC television network, as well as decreased tourism due to the [September 11 attacks](5058690.md). Disney earnings in fiscal 2001 were \$120 million compared with the previous year's \$920 million. To help reduce costs, Disney announced it would lay off 4,000 employees and close 300–400 Disney stores.[^cite_note-294][^cite_note-295] After winning the [World Series in 2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_World_Series), Disney sold the Anaheim Angels for \$180 million in 2003.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297] In 2003, Disney became the first studio to garner \$3 billion in a year at the box office.[^cite_note-298] The same year, Roy Disney announced his retirement because of how the company was being run, calling on Eisner to retire; the same week, board member [Stanley Gold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanley_Gold) retired for the same reasons. Gold and Disney formed the "Save Disney" campaign.[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300]

Gold letters

Disney bought *The Muppets* from the Jim Henson Company in 2004.

In 2004, at the company's annual meeting, the shareholders in a 43% vote voted Eisner out as chairman.[^cite_note-301] On March 4, [George J. Mitchell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_J._Mitchell), who was a member of the board, was named as replacement.[^cite_note-ChairmenofDisney-302] In April, Disney purchased the *Muppets* franchise from the Jim Henson Company for \$75 million, founding [Muppets Holding Company, LLC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Muppets_Studio).[^cite_note-303][^cite_note-304] Following the success of Disney-Pixar films *[Finding Nemo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finding_Nemo)* (2003), which became the highest-grossing animated film of all time at \$871 million during its initial release, and *[The Incredibles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Incredibles)* (2004),[^cite_note-305][^cite_note-306] Pixar looked for a new distributor once its deal with Disney ended in 2004.[^cite_note-307] Disney sold the loss-making Disney Stores chain of 313 stores to [Children's Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Children's_Place) on October 20.[^cite_note-308] Disney also sold the NHL team Mighty Ducks in 2005.[^cite_note-309] Roy E. Disney decided to rejoin the company and was given the role of consultant with the title "Director Emeritus".[^cite_note-310]

### 2005–2020: Bob Iger's first tenure, expansion, and Disney+

In March 2005, Bob Iger, president of the company, became CEO after Eisner's retirement in September; Iger was officially named head of the company on October 1.[^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312] Disney's eleventh theme park [Hong Kong Disneyland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Disneyland) opened on September 12, costing the company \$3.5 billion to construct.[^cite_note-313] On January 24, 2006, Disney began talks to acquire Pixar from [Steve Jobs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Jobs) for \$7.4 billion, and Iger appointed Pixar [chief creative officer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_creative_officer) (CCO) [John Lasseter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Lasseter) and president [Edwin Catmull](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edwin_Catmull) the heads of the [Walt Disney Animation Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Disney_Animation_Studios).[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315] A week later, Disney traded [ABC Sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN_on_ABC) commentator [Al Michaels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Michaels) to [NBCUniversal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBCUniversal), in exchange for the rights to Oswald the Lucky Rabbit and 26 cartoons featuring the character.[^cite_note-316] On February 6, the company announced it would be merging its ABC Radio networks and 22 stations with [Citadel Broadcasting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citadel_Broadcasting) in a \$2.7 billion deal, though which Disney acquired 52% of television broadcasting company [Citadel Communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citadel_Communications).[^cite_note-317][^cite_note-318] The Disney Channel movie *[High School Musical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_School_Musical)* aired and its soundtrack was certified triple [platinum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platinum_\(music_sales\)), becoming the first Disney Channel film to do so.[^cite_note-319]

Man in suit looking into the distance, with a blue background with words behind him

Bob Iger became CEO of Disney in 2005, expanding the company's properties

Disney's 2006 live-action film *[Pirates of the Caribbean: Dead Man's Chest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pirates_of_the_Caribbean:_Dead_Man's_Chest)* was Disney's biggest hit to that date and the third-highest-grossing film ever, making \$1 billion at the box office.[^cite_note-320] On June 28, the company announced it was replacing George Mitchell as chairman with a board members and former CEO of [P\&G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Procter_&_Gamble) [John E. Pepper Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_E._Pepper_Jr.)[^cite_note-ChairmenofDisney-302] The sequel *[High School Musical 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/High_School_Musical_2)* was released in 2007 on Disney Channel and broke several cable rating records.[^cite_note-321] In April 2007, the [Muppets Holding Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muppets_Holding_Company) was moved from Disney Consumer Products to the Walt Disney Studios division and renamed the [Muppets Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Muppets_Studio) to relaunch the division.[^cite_note-322][^cite_note-Muppets_2004-323] *[Pirates of the Caribbean: At World's End](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pirates_of_the_Caribbean:_At_World's_End)* became the highest-grossing film of 2007 at \$960 million.[^cite_note-324] Disney-Pixar films [*Ratatouille*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratatouille_\(film\)) (2007) and *[WALL-E](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WALL-E)* (2008) were a tremendous success, with *WALL-E* winning the Oscar for Best Animated Feature.[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327] After acquiring most of [Jetix Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jetix_Europe) through the acquisition of Fox Family Worldwide, Disney bought the remainder of the company in 2008 for \$318 million.[^cite_note-328]

Iger introduced [D23](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D23_\(Disney\)) in 2009 as Disney's official fan club.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330] In February, Disney announced a deal with [DreamWorks Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DreamWorks_Pictures) to distribute 30 of its films over the next five years through Touchstone Pictures, with Disney getting 10% of the gross.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332] The 2009 film *[Up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Up_\(2009_film\))* garnered Disney \$735 million at the box office, and the film won Best Animated Feature at the Academy Awards.[^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334] Later that year, Disney launched a television channel named [Disney XD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_XD), aimed at older children.[^cite_note-335] Disney bought [Marvel Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marvel_Entertainment) and its assets for \$4 billion in August, adding Marvel's comic-book characters to its merchandising line-up.[^cite_note-336] In September, Disney partnered with [News Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/News_Corporation) and NBCUniversal in a deal in which all parties would obtain 27% equity in streaming service [Hulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hulu), and Disney added ABC Family and Disney Channel to the streaming service.[^cite_note-337] On December 16, Roy E. Disney died of [stomach cancer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stomach_cancer); he was the last member of the Disney family to work for Disney.[^cite_note-338] In March 2010, [Haim Saban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haim_Saban) reacquired from Disney the *[Power Rangers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_Rangers)* franchise, including its 700-episode library, for around \$100 million.[^cite_note-339][^cite_note-340] Shortly after, Disney sold Miramax Films to an investment group headed by [Ronald Tutor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Tutor) for \$660 million.[^cite_note-341] During that time, Disney released the live-action *[Alice in Wonderland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice_in_Wonderland_\(2010_film\))* and the Disney-Pixar film *[Toy Story 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toy_Story_3)*, both of which grossed a little over \$1 billion, making them the sixth-and-seventh films to do so; and *Toy Story 3* became the first animated film to make over \$1 billion and the highest-grossing animated film. That year, Disney became the first studio to release two \$1-billion-dollar-earning films in one calendar year.[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343] In 2010, the company announced [ImageMovers Digital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ImageMovers_Digital), which it started in partnership with [ImageMovers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ImageMovers) in 2007, would be closing by 2011.[^cite_note-344]

[black letters spelling out the word Pixar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pixar)

Pixar had been making films with Disney from 1995 to 2005, until Disney bought them out in 2006 as one of its subsidiaries.

[Red background with white letters spelling out Marvel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marvel_Comics)

Marvel became a subsidiary of Disney in 2009 after Disney acquired them for \$4 billion.

[Black letters spelling out Lucasfilm in a slight arch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucasfilm)

After purchasing Lucasfilm in 2012, Disney vowed to make more *Star Wars* films.

[black logo with a big 20 on the left side and underlined words on the right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20th_Century_Studios)

In 2019, Disney's bought most of 21st Century Fox's assets for \$71 billion, rebranding some of them like the studio 20th Century Fox as 20th Century Studios.

The following year, Disney released its most recent traditionally animated film *[Winnie the Pooh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winnie_the_Pooh_\(2011_film\))* to theaters.[^cite_note-TraditionalAnimation-345] The release of *[Pirates of the Caribbean: On Stranger Tides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pirates_of_the_Caribbean:_On_Stranger_Tides)* garnered a little over \$1 billion, making it the eighth film to do so and Disney's highest-grossing film internationally, as well as the third-highest ever.[^cite_note-346] In January 2011, the size of [Disney Interactive Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Interactive_Studios) was reduced and 200 employees laid off.[^cite_note-347] In April, Disney began constructing its new theme park [Shanghai Disney Resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai_Disney_Resort), costing \$4.4 billion.[^cite_note-348] In August, Iger stated after the success of the [Pixar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pixar) and Marvel purchases, he and the Walt Disney Company were planning to "buy either new characters or businesses that are capable of creating great characters and great stories".[^cite_note-349] On October 30, 2012, Disney announced it would buy [Lucasfilm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucasfilm) for \$4.05 billion from [George Lucas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Lucas). Through the deal, Disney gained access to franchises such as *[Star Wars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Wars)*, for which Disney said it would make a new film for every two-to-three year, with the first being released in 2015. The deal gave Disney access to the *[Indiana Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indiana_Jones)* franchise, visual-effects studio [Industrial Light & Magic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_Light_&_Magic), and video game developer [LucasArts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LucasArts).[^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352]

In February 2012, Disney completed its acquisition of [UTV Software Communications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UTV_Software_Communications), expanding its market into India and the rest of Asia.[^cite_note-353] By March, Iger became Disney's chairman.[^cite_note-354] Marvel film *[The Avengers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Avengers_\(2012_film\))* became the third-highest-grossing film of all time with an initial-release gross of \$1.5 billion.[^cite_note-355] The company's 2013 releases include the Marvel film *[Iron Man 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iron_Man_3)*, which made over \$1.2 billion at the box office,[^cite_note-356] the prequel *[Monsters University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monsters_University)*, which made \$743 million at the box office, the Marvel film *[Thor: The Dark World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thor:_The_Dark_World)*, which garnered \$644 million at the box office, and Disney's animated film *[Frozen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frozen_\(2013_film\))*, which became the highest-grossing animated film of all time at \$1.2 billion.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-358] Merchandising for the film became so popular it made the company \$1 billion within a year, and a global shortage of merchandise for the film occurred.[^cite_note-359][^cite_note-360] In March 2013, Iger announced Disney had no 2D animation films in development, and a month, later the hand-drawn animation division was closed, and several veteran animators were laid off.[^cite_note-TraditionalAnimation-345] On March 24, 2014, Disney acquired [Maker Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maker_Studios), an active [multi-channel network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-channel_network) on [YouTube](3524766.md), for \$950 million.[^cite_note-361]

In June 2015, the company stated its consumer products and interactive divisions would merge to become new a subsidiary called [Disney Consumer Products and Interactive Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Consumer_Products_and_Interactive_Media).[^cite_note-362] In August, Marvel Studios was placed under the Walt Disney Studios division.[^cite_note-363] The company's 2015 releases include the successful animated film *[Inside Out](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inside_Out),* which grossed over \$858 million, and the Marvel film *[Avengers: Age of Ultron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avengers:_Age_of_Ultron)*, which grossed over \$1.4 billion.[^cite_note-364] *[Star Wars: The Force Awakens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Wars:_The_Force_Awakens)* was released and grossed over \$2 billion, making it the third-highest-grossing film of all time.[^cite_note-365] On April 4, 2016, Disney announced COO [Thomas O. Staggs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_O._Staggs), who was thought to be next in line after Iger, would leave in May, ending his 26-year career with Disney.[^cite_note-NYT-Mutual-366] Shanghai Disneyland opened on June 16, 2016, as the company's sixth theme-park resort.[^cite_note-367] In a move to start a streaming service, Disney bought 33% of the stock in [Major League Baseball](38776.md) technology company [Bamtech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bamtech) for \$1 billion in August.[^cite_note-368] In 2016, four Disney film releases made over \$1 billion; these were the animated film *[Zootopia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zootopia)*, Marvel film *[Captain America: Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captain_America:_Civil_War)*, Pixar film *[Finding Dory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finding_Dory),* and *[Rogue One: A Star Wars Story](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rogue_One:_A_Star_Wars_Story),* making Disney the first studio to surpass \$3 billion at the domestic box office.[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-370] Disney made an attempt to buy social media platform [Twitter](9988187.md) to market its content and merchandise but canceled the deal. Iger stated this was because he thought Disney would be taking on responsibilities it did not need and that it did not "feel Disney" to him.[^cite_note-371]

On March 23, 2017, Disney announced Iger had agreed to a one-year extension as CEO to July 2019, and to remain as a consultant for three years.[^cite_note-372][^cite_note-373] On August 8, 2017, Disney announced it would be ending its distribution deal with [Netflix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netflix), with the intent of launching its own streaming platform by 2019. During that time, Disney paid \$1.5 billion to acquire a 75% stake in BAMtech. Disney planned to start an ESPN streaming service with about "10,000 live regional, national, and international games and events a year" by 2018.[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] In November, CCO John Lasseter said he would take a 6-month absence because of "missteps", reported to be sexual misconduct allegations.[^cite_note-376] The same month, Disney and [21st Century Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/21st_Century_Fox) started negotiating a deal in which [Disney would acquire most of Fox's assets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acquisition_of_21st_Century_Fox_by_Disney).[^cite_note-377] Beginning in March 2018, a reorganization of the company led to the creation of business segments [Disney Parks, Experiences and Products](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Parks,_Experiences_and_Products) and [Direct-to-Consumer & International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Disney_Direct-to-Consumer_&_International). Parks & Consumer Products was primarily a merger of Parks & Resorts and Consumer Products & Interactive Media, while Direct-to-Consumer & International took over for Disney International and global sales, distribution, and streaming units from Disney-ABC TV Group and Studios Entertainment plus Disney Digital Network.[^cite_note-378] Iger described it as "strategically positioning our businesses" while according to *[The New York Times](30680.md)*, the reorganization was done in expectation of the 21st Century Fox purchase.[^cite_note-379]

In 2017, two of Disney's films had revenues of over \$1 billion; the live-action *[Beauty and the Beast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beauty_and_the_Beast_\(2017_film\))* and *[Star Wars: The Last Jedi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Wars:_The_Last_Jedi)*.[^cite_note-380][^cite_note-381] Disney launched subscription sports streaming service [ESPN+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN+) on April 12.[^cite_note-382] In June 2018, Lasseter's departure by the end of the year was announced; he would stay as a consultant until then.[^cite_note-383] To replace him; Disney promoted [Jennifer Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Lee_\(filmmaker\)), co-director of *Frozen* and co-writer of [Wreck-It Ralph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wreck-It_Ralph) (2012), as head of Walt Disney Animation Studios; and [Pete Docter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Docter), who had been with Pixar since 1990 and directed Up, Monster&#x73;*, Inc.*, and *Inside Out*, as head of Pixar.[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385] [Comcast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comcast) offered to buy 21st Century Fox for \$65 billion over Disney's \$51 billion bid but withdrew its offer after Disney countered with a \$71 billion bid. Disney obtained [antitrust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AntiTrust) approval from the [United States Department of Justice](52563.md) to acquire Fox.[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] Disney again made \$7 billion at the box office with three film that made \$1 billion; Marvel films *[Black Panther](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Panther_\(film\)) and [Avengers: Infinity War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avengers:_Infinity_War)*—the latter taking over \$2 billion and becoming the fifth-highest-grossing film ever— and Pixar film *[Incredibles 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incredibles_2)*.[^cite_note-388][^cite_note-389]

blue letters with a plus sign at the end and an arch above the letters

Disney's video streaming subscription service Disney+ was launched in 2019, which has a total of over 135 million subscriptions as of June 2022.

On March 20, 2019, Disney acquired 21st Century Fox's assets for \$71 billion from [Rupert Murdoch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rupert_Murdoch), making it the biggest acquisition in Disney's history. After the purchase, *The New York Times* described Disney as "an entertainment colossus the size of which the world has never seen".[^cite_note-390] Through the acquisition, Disney gained [20th Century Fox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20th_Century_Fox); [20th Century Fox Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20th_Century_Fox_Television); [Fox Searchlight Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Searchlight_Pictures); [National Geographic Partners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic_Partners); [Fox Networks Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Networks_Group); Indian television broadcaster [Star India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_India); streaming service [Hotstar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney+_Hotstar); and a 30% stake in Hulu, bringing its ownership on Hulu to 60%. [Fox Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Corporation) and its assets were excluded from the deal because of antitrust laws.[^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392] Disney became the first film studio to have seven films gross \$1 billion: Marvel's *[Captain Marvel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captain_Marvel_\(film\)),* the live action *[Aladdin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aladdin_\(2019_film\)),* Pixar's *[Toy Story 4](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toy_Story_4),* the CGI remake of *[The Lion King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Lion_King_\(2019_film\)), [Star Wars: The Rise of Skywalker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Wars:_The_Rise_of_Skywalker),* and the highest-grossing film of all time up to that point at \$2.8 billion *[Avengers: Endgame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avengers:_Endgame)*.[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-394] On November 12, Disney launched the [Disney+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney+) streaming service in the U.S., Canada and the Netherlands; At launch the service had 500 movies and 7,500 episodes of television shows from Disney, Pixar, Marvel, *Star Wars*, [National Geographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Geographic), and other brands. Within the first day, the streaming platform had over 10 million subscriptions; and by 2022 it had over 135 million and was available in over 190 countries.[^cite_note-395][^cite_note-396] At the beginning of 2020, Disney removed the Fox name from its assets, rebranding them as 20th Century Studios and Searchlight Pictures.[^cite_note-397]

### 2020–2026: Bob Chapek's leadership, COVID-19 pandemic, Iger's return, reorganization, and 100th anniversary

Further information: [Impact of the COVID-19 pandemic on the Walt Disney Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic_on_the_Walt_Disney_Company)

[Bob Chapek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Chapek), who had been with the company for 18 years and was chairman of Disney Parks, Experiences and Products, became CEO after Iger resigned on February 25, 2020. Iger said he would stay as an [Executive chairman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_chairman) until December 31, 2021, to help with its creative strategy.[^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399] In April, Iger resumed operational duties as executive chairman to help the company during the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), and Chapek was appointed to the board of directors.[^cite_note-400][^cite_note-401] During the pandemic, Disney temporarily closed all its theme parks, delayed the release of several movies, and stopped all cruises.[^cite_note-402][^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404] Due to the closures, Disney announced it would stop paying 100,000 employees but still provide healthcare benefits, and urged U.S. employees to apply for government benefits, saving the company \$500 million a month. Iger gave up his \$47 million salary and Chapek took a 50% salary reduction.[^cite_note-405]

In the company's second fiscal quarter of 2020, Disney reported a \$1.4 billion loss, with a fall in earnings of 91% to \$475 million from the previous year's \$5.4 billion.[^cite_note-406] By August, two-thirds of the company was owned by large financial institutions.[^cite_note-2/3Stock-407] In September, the company dismissed 28,000 employees, 67% of whom were part-time, from its Parks, Experiences and Products division. Chairman of the division [Josh D'Amaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josh_D'Amaro) wrote; "We initially hoped that this situation would be short-lived, and that we would recover quickly and return to normal. Seven months later, we find that has not been the case." Disney lost \$4.7 billion in its fiscal third quarter of 2020.[^cite_note-408] In November, Disney laid off another 4,000 employees, raising the total to 32,000 employees.[^cite_note-409] The following month, Disney named Alan Bergman as chairman of its Disney Studios Content division to oversee its film studios.[^cite_note-410] As part of their post-21st Century Fox acquisition operation consolidations, [Touchstone Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Touchstone_Television) ceased operations in December,[^cite_note-411] Disney announced in March 2021 it would be launching a new division called [20th Television Animation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20th_Television_Animation) to focus on mature audiences,[^cite_note-412] and Disney closed [Blue Sky Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_Sky_Studios) in April 2021, and Blue Sky Studios was succeeded by [20th Century Animation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20th_Century_Animation).[^cite_note-413] Later that month, Disney and [Sony](26989.md) agreed a multi-year licensing deal that would give Disney access to Sony's films from 2022 to 2026 to televise or stream on Disney+ once Sony's deal with Netflix ended.[^cite_note-414] Although it performed poorly at the box office because of COVID-19, Disney's animated film *[Encanto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encanto)* (2021) was one of the biggest hits during the pandemic, with its song "[We Don't Talk About Bruno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/We_Don't_Talk_About_Bruno)" topping the U.S. [Billboard Hot 100](423161.md) charts.[^cite_note-415][^cite_note-416]

After Iger's term as executive chairman ended on December 31, he announced he would resign as chairman. The company brought in an operating executive at [the Carlyle Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Carlyle_Group) and former board member [Susan Arnold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Susan_Arnold) as Disney's first female chairperson.[^cite_note-417] On March 10, Disney suspended operations in Russia because of [Russia's invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md), and was the first major Hollywood studio to halt release of a major picture due to Russia's invasion; other movie studios followed.[^cite_note-418] In March 2022, around 60 employees protested [the company's silence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_and_Florida's_Parental_Rights_in_Education_Act) on the [Florida Parental Rights in Education Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florida_Parental_Rights_in_Education_Act) that was dubbed the Don't Say Gay Bill, and prohibits non-age-appropriate classroom instruction on [sexual orientation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_orientation) and [gender identity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gender_identity) in Florida's public-school districts. The protest was dubbed the "Disney Do Better Walkout"; employees protested near a Disney Studios lot, and other employees voiced their concerns through social media. Employees called on Disney to stop campaign contributions to Florida politicians who supported the bill, to help protect employees from it, and to stop construction at Walt Disney World in Florida. Chapek responded by stating the company had made a mistake by staying silent and said; "We pledge our ongoing support of the LGBTQ+ community".[^cite_note-419][^cite_note-420] Amid Disney's response to the bill, the [Florida Legislature](429346.md) passed a bill to remove Disney's quasi-government district Reedy Creek.[^cite_note-421]

On June 28, Disney's board members unanimously agreed to give Chapek a three-year contract extension.[^cite_note-422] In August, [Disney Streaming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Streaming) exceeded Netflix in total subscriptions with 221 million subscribers compared to Netflix's 220 million.[^cite_note-423]

On November 20, 2022, Iger accepted the position of Disney's CEO after Chapek was dismissed following poor earnings performance and decisions unpopular with other executives.[^cite_note-424][^cite_note-la_times_megjames-425] The board announced Iger would serve for two years with a mandate to develop a strategy for renewed growth and help identify a successor.[^cite_note-426]

In November 2022, a group of [YouTube TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/YouTube_TV) subscribers in four states filed a [class-action](7200.md) antitrust lawsuit against Disney, alleging that Disney's control of both ESPN and Hulu allowed the company to "inflate prices marketwise by raising the prices of its own products" and by requiring streaming services including YouTube TV and [Sling TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sling_TV) to include ESPN in base packages, forcing subscribers to pay more for subscriptions than they would in a competitive market.[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428]

In January 2023, Disney announced that [Mark Parker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Parker) would replace Arnold as the company's chairperson.[^cite_note-429] In February 2023, Disney announced that it would be cutting \$5.5 billion in costs, which includes eliminating 7,000 jobs representing 3% of its workforce. Disney reorganized into three divisions: [Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Entertainment), ESPN, and Parks, Experiences and Products.[^cite_note-430] In April 2023, Disney implemented the second and largest wave of job cuts, affecting Disney Entertainment, ESPN, and the Parks, Experiences and Products division. This move was part of the plan to cut costs by \$5.5 billion.[^cite_note-431]



Disney's promotional logo for its centennial.

In 2023, Disney began its "100 Years of Wonder" campaign in celebration of the centennial anniversary of the company's founding. This included a new animated centennial logo intro for the Walt Disney Pictures division, a touring exhibition, events at the parks and a commemorative commercial that aired during [Super Bowl LVII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LVII).[^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433]

In October 2023, Disney announced its entrance into sports betting through a partnership with [Penn Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penn_Entertainment), launching the ESPN Bet app, despite internal debates and concerns over brand image. This move marked a significant pivot from Iger's earlier stance against gambling, driven by the potential to attract younger audiences and secure a financial future for ESPN, amidst declining traditional television viewership and increasing online sports gambling revenue.[^cite_note-434] In November 2023, Disney shortened the lengthy name of Disney Parks, Experiences and Products to Disney Experiences.[^cite_note-2023Rebrand-435]

In February 2024, Debra O'Connell, a longtime executive at Disney, was appointed president of a new news division that would include [ABC News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_News_\(United_States\)) and local stations. O'Connell is responsible for ABC News's signature properties, including *[Good Morning America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Morning_America)* and *[World News Tonight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABC_World_News_Tonight)*. It will serve as an intermediary between Dana Walden, co-chair of Disney Entertainment and [Kim Godwin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kim_Godwin), the ABC News president. Other online news units have similar processes.[^cite_note-436] In February, Walt Disney and Reliance Industries announced the merger of their India TV and streaming media assets.[^cite_note-437]

In February 2024, The Walt Disney Company finalized a \$1.5 billion equity investment in [Epic Games](573199.md) to develop a persistent universe operating within *[Fortnite](57813623.md)* and [Unreal Engine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unreal_Engine). The partnership integrates Disney's [intellectual property](14724.md), including Marvel, *Star Wars*, and Pixar, into a [digital ecosystem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_ecosystem) connected to Fortnite where users can engage with content and purchase digital goods. The initiative serves as the company's largest expansion into the [video game industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_game_industry), citing the growing amount of time younger audiences spend playing video games as a key factor behind the investment.[^cite_note-438]

In July 2024, Ryan Mitchell Kramer, a Californian man, hacked and leaked over a [terabyte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Byte) of the company's [Slack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slack_\(software\)) messages while pretending to be part of a hacktivist group "Nullbulge". Kramer managed to get access to the company's accounts by using a Trojan to steal the login credentials of the employee's work and personal accounts. Kramer claimed that the motive for the breach was the group's dislike of [art generated by artificial intelligence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_intelligence_art), though it was later discovered that Kramer tried to extort the employees.[^cite_note-439][^cite_note-440]

Members of [Generation Z](4015968.md) were absent from the D23 fan event held in August 2024 in Anaheim, which was dominated by [millennials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Millennials) representing all 50 [US states](18618239.md) and 36 countries.[^cite_note-Beckett-441] Disney chief brand officer Asad Ayaz pushed back against the idea that this was a symptom of a broader trend: "Our [fandoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fandom) and our fans and different generations show up in different ways".[^cite_note-Beckett-441] Theme park experts noted that the true test of the enduring power of the Disney brand will be whether Generation Z takes [Generation Alpha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generation_Alpha) to Disney theme parks.[^cite_note-Beckett-441]

In September 2024, Disney announced [Adam Smith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Smith_\(media_executive\)) would succeed [Aaron LaBerge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aaron_LaBerge) as Chief Product and Technology Officer for Disney Entertainment and ESPN.[^cite_note-442]

In October 2024, Disney announced [James P. Gorman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_P._Gorman) would replace Mark Parker as chairman in January 2025. It also announced a successor to CEO Bob Iger would be named in early 2026.[^cite_note-443] The company's 2024 releases include the Pixar film *[Inside Out 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inside_Out_2)*, which became the highest grossing animated film of all time with \$1.69 billion, the Marvel film *[Deadpool & Wolverine](52234178.md)*, which made over \$1.3 billion at the box office, the animated film *[Moana 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moana_2)*, which grossed a little over \$1 billion at the box office, and *[Mufasa: The Lion King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mufasa:_The_Lion_King)*, which grossed \$723 million at the box office.

On May 7, 2025, Disney announced its seventh resort, [Disneyland Abu Dhabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disneyland_Abu_Dhabi), planned for [Yas Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yas_Island). Similar to [Tokyo Disney Resort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokyo_Disney_Resort), it will not be owned or managed by Disney, but instead by [Miral Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miral_Group).[^cite_note-Abu_Dhabi_announcement-444][^cite_note-445]

In September 2025, the [American Broadcasting Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Broadcasting_Company) (ABC) [indefinitely suspended](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suspension_of_Jimmy_Kimmel_Live!) production of the late-night talk show *[Jimmy Kimmel Live!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Kimmel_Live!)*, sparking widespread backlash from political leaders and commentators,[^cite_note-446][^cite_note-447] entertainers, entertainment industry unions,[^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449][^cite_note-450] constitutional scholars and the public, as well as boycotts against ABC and The Walt Disney Company.[^cite_note-451][^cite_note-452] After a week of suspension since September 17, on September 22 Disney announced that Kimmel's suspension was lifted,[^cite_note-453] and the show resumed broadcasting on September 23.[^cite_note-454] Prior to the broadcast, President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) wrote, on his Truth Social platform, “I think we’re going to test ABC out on this. Let’s see how we do.” Lawyer [Roberta Kaplan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberta_Kaplan), representing shareholders [American Federation of Teachers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Federation_of_Teachers) and [Reporters Without Borders](526054.md), stated that major media companies “should not succumb to unconstitutional threats or blackmail” and that a “credible basis to suspect” the board of breaching its fiduciary duty, by prioritizing “improper political and affiliate considerations.”[^cite_note-455]

On December 11, 2025, Disney agreed to acquire an equity stake in [OpenAI](48795986.md) for \$1 billion. Simultaneously, the two companies also signed a three-year agreement to allow users to generate videos using OpenAI's [Sora](76106601.md) video platform and licensed Disney characters including Mickey Mouse and Cinderella.[^cite_note-456][^cite_note-457] The deal, however, was cancelled on March 24, 2026, when OpenAI announced that its Sora model would be shut down.[^cite_note-458]

### 2026–present: Josh D'Amaro's leadership

On February 3, 2026, The Walt Disney Company's board of directors announced the unanimous election of [Josh D'Amaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Josh_D'Amaro) as the company's next chief executive officer (CEO), succeeding Bob Iger effective March 18, 2026.[^cite_note-D'Amaro_Named_Next_CEO-PR-459] D’Amaro, a 28-year veteran of the company and former Chairman of Disney Experiences, will also join the board of directors following the company's annual meeting. Simultaneously, [Dana Walden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dana_Walden) was appointed to the newly created role of President and Chief Creative Officer (CCO), reporting directly to D'Amaro. Iger is set to remain with the company as a senior advisor and board member until his official retirement on December 31, 2026.[^cite_note-460]

## Company units

For a more comprehensive list, see [List of assets owned by the Walt Disney Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_assets_owned_by_the_Walt_Disney_Company) and [List of libraries owned by the Walt Disney Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_libraries_owned_by_the_Walt_Disney_Company).

The Walt Disney Company operates three primary business segments:[^cite_note-DisneyLeadership-461]

- **[Disney Entertainment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Entertainment)** oversees the company's full portfolio of non-sports entertainment media and content businesses globally, including the [Walt Disney Studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Disney_Studios_\(division\)), [Disney Entertainment Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Entertainment_Television), [Disney Streaming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Streaming) and [Disney Platform Distribution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Platform_Distribution). The division is led by [Dana Walden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dana_Walden).

- **[ESPN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ESPN,_LLC)**, also referred to as **Sports**,

  [^cite_note-462]

  &#x20;is responsible for the management and supervision of the company's portfolio of sports content, products, and experiences across all of Disney's platforms worldwide, including its international sports channels. The division is led by [James Pitaro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Pitaro).

- **[Disney Experiences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Experiences)** is responsible for theme parks and resorts, cruise and vacation experiences, and consumer products such as toys, apparel, and books. The division is led by Thomas Mazloum.

## Awards and nominations

See also: [List of Academy Awards for Walt Disney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Academy_Awards_for_Walt_Disney)

As of 2022, the Walt Disney Company has won 135 Academy Awards, [32 of them were awarded to Walt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Academy_Awards_for_Walt_Disney). The company has won 16 Academy Awards for Best Animated Short Film, 16 for Best Original Song, 15 for Best Animated Feature, 11 for Best Original Score, 5 for [Best Documentary Feature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Documentary_Feature_Film), 5 for [Best Visual Effects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award_for_Best_Visual_Effects), and several others as well special awards.[^cite_note-466] Disney has also won 29 Golden Globe Awards, 51 [British Academy of Film and Television Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Academy_of_Film_and_Television_Arts) (BAFTA) awards, and 36 Grammy Awards as of 2022.[^cite_note-467][^cite_note-468][^cite_note-469][^cite_note-488]

## Legacy

The Walt Disney Company is one of the world's largest entertainment companies and is considered to be a pioneer in the animation industry, having produced 790 features, 122 of which are animated films.[^cite_note-489][^cite_note-490] Many of its films are considered to be the [greatest of all time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_films_considered_the_best), including *Pinocchio*, *Toy Story*, *Bambi*, *Ratatouille*, *Snow White and the Seven Dwarfs*, and *Mary Poppins*.[^cite_note-491][^cite_note-492][^cite_note-493] Disney has also created some of the most influential and memorable characters of all time, such as [Mickey Mouse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mickey_Mouse), [Woody](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woody_\(Toy_Story\)), [Captain America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Rogers_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe\)) ([MCU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marvel_Cinematic_Universe)), [Jack Sparrow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Sparrow), [Iron Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tony_Stark_\(Marvel_Cinematic_Universe\)) (MCU), and [Elsa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elsa_\(Frozen\)).[^cite_note-TimeMickeyHistory-26][^cite_note-494][^cite_note-495]

Disney has been recognized for revolutionizing the animation industry; according to *[Den of Geek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Den_of_Geek)*, the risk of making the first animated feature *Snow White and the Seven Dwarfs* has "changed cinema".[^cite_note-496] The company, mainly through Walt, has introduced new technologies and more-advanced techniques for animating, as well as adding personalities to characters.[^cite_note-497][^cite_note-DisneyAnimation-141] Some of Disney's technological innovations for animation include invention of the multiplane camera, xerography, CAPS, deep canvas, and [RenderMan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RenderMan).[^cite_note-DisneyInventions-220] Many songs from the company's films have become extremely popular, and several have peaked at number one on [Billboard's](18309966.md) [Hot 100](423161.md).[^cite_note-498] Some songs from the *Silly Symphony* series became immensely popular across the US.[^cite_note-SillySymphonies-35]

Disney has been ranked number 48 in the 2023 *[Fortune 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500)* list of the largest United States corporations by total revenue and fourth in Fortune's 2022 "World's Most Admired Companies".[^cite_note-Fortune500-2][^cite_note-499] According to [*Smithsonian Magazine*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smithsonian_\(magazine\)), there are "few symbols of pure Americana more potent than the Disney theme parks", which are "well-established cultural icons", with the company name and Mickey Mouse being "household names".[^cite_note-500] Disney is one of the biggest competitors in the theme park industry with 12 parks, all of which were the top-25 most-visited parks in 2018. Disney theme parks worldwide had over 157 million visitors, making it the most-visited theme-park company in the world, doubling the attendance number of the second-most-visited company. Of the 157 million visitors, the Magic Kingdom had 20.8 million of the guests, making it the most-visited theme park in the world.[^cite_note-501][^cite_note-502] In 2022, referring to the time when Disney first entered the theme park industry, [CNN](62028.md) stated: "It changed an already legendary company. And it changed the entire theme park industry."[^cite_note-503] According to *[The Orange County Register](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Orange_County_Register)*, Walt Disney World has "changed entertainment by showing how a theme park could help make a company into a lifestyle brand".[^cite_note-504]

## Criticism and controversies

Main article: [Criticism of the Walt Disney Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criticism_of_the_Walt_Disney_Company)

The Walt Disney Company has been criticized for making purportedly sexist and racist content in the past, putting [LGBT+](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT+) elements in its films, and not having enough LGBT+ representation.[^cite_note-505][^cite_note-506] There have been controversies over alleged [plagiarism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plagiarism),[^cite_note-507] poor pay and working conditions,[^cite_note-508] and poor treatment of animals.[^cite_note-509] Disney has also been criticized for filming in the autonomous region of [Xinjiang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinjiang), where [human rights abuses are taking place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xinjiang_internment_camps).[^cite_note-510]

### Racism

Several of Disney's films have been considered to be racist; one of the company's most controversial films, *[Song of the South](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Song_of_the_South),* was criticized for portraying racial stereotypes. For that reason, the film was never released to home video in the United States or Disney+.[^cite_note-511] Other characters that have been called racist are Sunflower, a black [centaurette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centaurides) who serves a white centaurette in *Fantasia*; the Siamese cats in *Lady and the Tramp*, who are considered to be overexaggerated as Asians; stereotypes of Native Americans in *Peter Pan*; and crows in *Dumbo*, who are depicted as African Americans who use [jive talk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jive_talk), with their leader being named Jim Crow, believed to be in reference to [racial segregation laws in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jim_Crow_laws).[^cite_note-512][^cite_note-513] When watching a film on Disney+ considered to have wrongful racist stereotypes, Disney added a disclaimer before the film starts to help avoid controversies.[^cite_note-514]

### Plagiarism

Disney has also been accused a number of times of plagiarizing already existing works in its films. Most notably, *The Lion King* has many similarities in its characters and events to an animated series called *[Kimba the White Lion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kimba_the_White_Lion)* by animator [Osamu Tezuka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Osamu_Tezuka).[^cite_note-515] *[Atlantis: The Lost Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantis:_The_Lost_Empire)* also has many similarities to the [anime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anime) show *[Nadia: The Secret of Blue Water](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nadia:_The_Secret_of_Blue_Water)* that were considered so prevalent the latter show's creator [Gainax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gainax) was planning to sue Disney but was stopped by its series' network [NHK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NHK).[^cite_note-516] Kelly Wilson, creator of the short *The Snowman* (2014), filed two lawsuits, one which came after the first was rescinded, against Disney for [copyright infringement](18948365.md) in Disney's animated film *[Frozen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frozen_\(2013_film\))*. Disney later settled the lawsuit with Wilson, allowing the company to create a sequel to *Frozen*.[^cite_note-517] Screenwriter [Gary L. Goldman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Goldman_\(screenwriter\)) sued Disney over its film *[Zootopia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zootopia)*, claiming he had earlier pitched an identical, same-titled story to the company. A judge dismissed the lawsuit, stating there was not enough evidence to prove any plagiarism.[^cite_note-518]

On 10 December 2025, Disney sent Google a cease-and-desist letter, accusing Google of copyright infringement, claiming that Google's AI models have generated characters that resemble those from iconic Disney shows like Frozen, Deadpool, Star Wars, and more.[^cite_note-519]

### LGBT+ representation

Disney has been criticized for both putting LGBT+ elements into its films and for having insufficient LGBT+ representation in its media. In the live-action film *Beauty and the Beast*, director [Bill Condon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bill_Condon) announced [LeFou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Fou_\(Disney\)) would be depicted as a gay character, prompting Kuwait, Malaysia, and a theater in Alabama to ban the film, and Russia to give it a stricter rating.[^cite_note-520] In Russia and several Middle Eastern countries, the Pixar movie *[Onward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Onward_\(film\))* was banned for having Disney's first openly lesbian character Officer Specter, while others said Disney needed more representation of LGBT+ persons in its media.[^cite_note-521][^cite_note-522] Because of a scene featuring two lesbians kissing, Pixar's *[Lightyear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lightyear_\(film\))* was banned in 13 predominantly Muslim countries.[^cite_note-523][^cite_note-524] In a leaked video of a Disney meeting, participants talked about pushing LGBT+ themes in the company's media, angering some people, including in aforementioned countries, who say the company is "trying to sexualize children", while others applauded its actions.[^cite_note-525]

### Sexism

Some [Disney Princess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disney_Princess) films have been considered to be sexist toward women. [Snow White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snow_White_\(Disney_character\)) is said to be too worried about her appearance while [Cinderella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinderella_\(Disney_character\)) is deemed to have no talents. [Aurora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurora_\(Sleeping_Beauty\)) is also said to be weak because she is always waiting to be rescued. In some of the princess films, men have more dialogue, and there are more speaking male characters than female. Disney's more-recent films are considered to be less sexist than its earlier films.[^cite_note-526]

### Working conditions

Disney has been accused of having poor working conditions. A protest by 2,000 workers at Disneyland in 2022 accused the company of poor pay at an average of \$13 an hour, with some saying they were evicted from their homes.[^cite_note-527] In 2010, at a factory in China where Disney products were being made, workers experienced working hours three times longer than those prescribed by law, and one of the workers died by suicide.[^cite_note-528]

### Animal cruelty

In 1990, Disney paid \$95,000 to avoid legal action over 16 [animal-cruelty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cruelty_to_animals) charges for beating vultures to death, shooting at birds, and starving some birds at [Discovery Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discovery_Island_\(Bay_Lake\)). The company took these actions because the birds were attacking other animals and taking their food.[^cite_note-529] When Animal Kingdom first opened, there were concerns about the animals because several of them died. Animal rights groups protested, but the [United States Department of Agriculture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Agriculture) found no violations of animal-welfare regulations.[^cite_note-530]
